# PROGRESS

Single source of truth for what is done, in flight and next. Updated by the orchestrator after every `/plan`, `/implement`, `/launch`. Keep entries to one line; details live in `docs/tasks/`.

Feature-complete; no new features before the release (James, 2026-10-31: "make sure the ones we have work reliably, without bug").

**Known issues shipped with v0.1 (follow-ups after the release):**
- (fixed before the release: T-0215 withdraws `google_search` when search is off; GPU check T-0220 2026-11-01: shared used_browser 9/9 vs 1/9 before, 'withdrawal unblocks the handoff')
- The Brave API fallback can carry a lone surrogate from its JSON into the next LLM request; that turn ends in an error, the session continues (T-0216c follow-up).
- Settings panel: a first Stop and apply pressed while listening leaves later presses dead until the turn ends (T-0213 R-25); a second change made in the one round trip before the page reads the echo is refused as stale (T-0218 R-2); outside live, a restart hint can still name a flag already in force (T-0217 R-29); `--max-tool-steps N` is a template.
- `test_service_ports_unreachable_with_allow_list` fails when other processes probe the real service ports during the run (T-0218 R-1); run it on an idle box.
- A long self-doubting thought ('Wait, ...') can run into the LLM timeout; the turn then ends with an error and the user hears nothing (T-0226 B, 1 of 39 turns); follow-up: an honest-ending clip on that path.
- ReGround (off by default) sends no pass 2 when the shared screen changed during the turn (seen in T-0227's A-6 rig); follow-up after the release.
- Raising the 4-image ceiling is a launcher change (`services/vllm.sh` `--limit-mm-per-prompt` + `Budget.max_images`) with a GPU bench; planned after the release (James, 2026-11-01: not tonight; README explains it).
- Serial `make fast` takes about 16 min against its 120 s ceiling; `make fast-parallel` about 90 s under load.

**Waiting on James:** desk checks (voice end to end with the settings panel; the café-machine handoff with search on; Blender; three.js M1); the T-0215 ruling; deleting branches `scratch-t0211-merge-check T-0210-b-trialA t0210-b-tests3-trialA`; GPU runs still queued, none blocking (T-0181 C, T-0183 B, T-0179 C, at_checkout clip render).

Groups A (machine) + B (prompt) concurrent, C (session) after, D `/launch` (two real frames, a question needing both, the first measured visual-token cost of a screen). - **T-0056 the HUD looks like 2026, and the diff proves it changed nothing else** (planned 2026-09-16, the human: "can you make the UI more 2026? ... This shouldn't impact core features, right? just html, css, js"): two groups, A the page chrome (`index.html`, `styles.css`), B the generated surface themed by binding the ~60 `--a2ui-*` properties the vendored renderer already reads. **No collision with T-0047** — its criterion 16 names these two files as untouched, so they run concurrently. Three measured findings drive it: `.pane` and `<main>` are a **cross-file contract** three panels mount into, so a wrapper div or a grid written for exactly today's panes silently un-grids them; `var(--fg, …)` and `var(--mono, …)` are **referenced and never declared**, so the surface and the log path have used hard-coded fallbacks since T-0024 — a resolving fallback is exactly how a rename hides from every test; and two real contrast failures, the empty-transcript line at **1.34:1** (invisible) and `[data-unplayed]` at **2.94:1**, where a naive pass would break the strike-through rule T-0030 pins. The loopback rule is the headline risk: a font CDN, icon set or framework stylesheet would fail the zero-non-loopback test, so nothing is fetched and font stacks are system-only. Screenshots of the page as it stands are in the scratchpad.
- **T-0055 the model writes the surface in its reply, not in its thinking** (**MERGED 2026-09-17**, d80a456; structural criteria reviewed and verified, the `/tokenize` count and the placement eval still owed under `/launch`; block 1 870 code points, offline token estimate 500 with ~100 under the ceiling; planned 2026-09-16; the human ruled the approach: "only solvable by prompting a priori for us — we don't want to extract such things from that channel ourselves"): asked for a flight booking interface at 21:52, the model wrote a **complete, correctly fenced, root-bearing** surface of 15 components entirely inside its **reasoning** channel, then wrote `I will say "I have created a flight booking interface for you."` and said exactly that. No `ui.gate` record exists anywhere in that log. It complied with every instruction it had: the prefix's only placement rule is *"Never leave the **answer** inside the thinking channel"*, and the line it followed most literally is *"Open and close the fence in the same message"*, which it did. Two findings from the pinned chat template shape the fix: the template's own `strip_thinking` macro **deletes** the thought span when history is re-rendered, so "what you write while thinking is erased" is literally true and is the strongest sentence available; and the channel delimiters are **special tokens** (ids 98/100/101/105/106), so quoting one into the block would encode a control token inside the cached system turn — the obvious wrong move, now forbidden by a fast-suite row. **Necessary but not sufficient:** in that same log the thought-fence *itself* straddled canvases 1 → 2, so T-0052 must merge first or the tail would simply be spoken as JSON instead. Block has 221 code points of headroom; the eval is 2 arms × 7 cases × n=5 and has never run.
- **T-0052 a surface longer than one canvas is reassembled, and JSON can never reach the voice** (**MERGED 2026-09-16**, 723275d; high; two concurrent groups A `llm_client` / B `speech_gate`, C `session.py` blocked → **renumbered T-0054**): the flight-booking turn at the desk split one A2UI fence across two canvases, so `split_segments` was called with a constant `in_ui=False` per canvas and its return value discarded; canvas 1 carried 409 chars of `ui`, canvas 2 carried 902 chars of `answer`, and **807 chars of JSON were spoken aloud**. A: carry the fence state across canvases and close it structurally where the JSON ends (a depth/string scanner, proved on the real payload: it cuts at 1159 of 1311 chars, leaving the human's closing sentence intact), bounded by `UI_FENCE_MAX_CANVASES` / `UI_FENCE_MAX_CHARS`. B: a narrow voice guard in the speech gate, with an adversarial row pinning that prose containing braces and the word "component" is still spoken. Naive repair alone would have made this turn **silent** — no closing marker was ever emitted (ruling A1). Tests written and activated (cc66565): 33 red rows across four modules, all `AssertionError`, plus 14 preservation rows that are green and whose job is to stay green. **Two implementers dispatched 2026-09-16**, concurrent. The test-author raised six problems with the task rather than working around any of them and all six were accepted, two changing the spec: criterion 9's refusal predicate never fired on the very string it was written to refuse (ruling A8), and the density rule cannot stand alone because the speech gate's Hypothesis row draws from an alphabet holding every structural character except the brace, so a bare density rule makes a settled property test flaky rather than red (ruling A9). Measured: the gate rejects canvas 2's 750-char slice outright, so the emission shape is the growing carry (A10); canvas 1 alone renders **two** components, the reassembly renders **seven** (A6). **Both groups implemented 2026-09-16, four reviewers in flight** (two per group, high blast radius). Group B: all rows green, `ruff`/`mypy` clean. Group A: 81 of 84 rows green; the three red are **test bugs, each verified independently** — a row pinning the per-canvas slice against ruling A10's growing carry (A16), an expectation containing a space no reachable value can hold (A17), and a fixture the real gate rejects on its own because its only component is not named `root` (A18). **Ordering constraint (A19): group A does not merge alone** — after an abandoned carry every later JSON-looking canvas is classified `answer` again, so a runaway fence delivers JSON to the speech gate, and group B's guard is the defence for that exact path — **both reviewers confirmed it by construction**. **Both groups are now assembled on `T-0052-fence-carry` and the full fast suite is GREEN on the combination: 2315 passed, 0 failed** (serial, 219.9 s, 2026-09-16, several agents on the box). That is the first time the two have been in one tree. With the verifier. **Group A reviewed twice, implementation accepted both times**; the scanner survived 11 110 exhaustive short inputs, 200 000 random strings and 120 000 streaming sequences with zero raises, zero hangs, and the fence marker never once in a spoken piece. What came back was **five test gaps, all correct code with a blind suite** (A25, A26, A28): the depth clamp its own row claims to pin is never exercised and losing it makes the turn silent; the written-versus-inferred distinction has no coverage because every recorded case puts the marker flush against the JSON; the escape rule's own fixture cannot discriminate it; a held partial marker that never completes is dropped by an untested clause; and — the highest-value one — removing the empty-remainder deferral **recreates the reported bug**, speaking a canvas of JSON, because interface surfaces are concatenated top-level messages so a boundary landing on `}}` is an ordinary shape. **Ruling A17 was corrected by A29**: I had written that the closing-braces row still pinned its property, and it does not. **Group B returned REVISE and ESCALATE** — see A20-A24. Rows in flight for all of it.
- **T-0053 a memory is never presented as something the assistant can see** — **groups A and B MERGED 2026-09-17 at 4fcade7** (2432 passed on 91f6379; the origin query returns zero hits against a copy of the human's real store; criterion 24 owed under `/launch`; group C `session.py` held). Earlier: (planned 2026-09-16; groups A `prompt_builder` / B `memory/store` concurrent, C `session.py` blocked): a fresh session with **nothing shared** asked for "a flat looking UI" and got a surface about vector calculus, because the prompt carried three captions of an hour-old textbook under a bare `[memory]` header, in the user message, with **zero `ImagePart`s** — and the model's own thought reads "based on the provided screenshots of a calculus textbook page". A: every recollection is dated and marked as a recollection, and a prompt with no image in it says so. B: retrieval stops recalling on a stopword — measured, `"Make a flat looking UI."` returns all three episodes at score **exactly 0.0**, matched on `('a',)`, so bm25 IDF contributed nothing and recency alone ranked them; `session_id` column plus a `user_version` migration of the human's real database. Group A takes **two reviewers by exception**: the over-wide direction makes the assistant deny it can see a screen the human is sharing, which is worse than the bug. Six ambiguities for the human (recall horizon, past-tense captions, suppress-vs-label, `session_id`, the relevance constant, the system prefix).

- **T-0158, `down` stops the STT container.** Merged 2026-10-26 at 39c7cfb (two reviews; verified at 37d4b4c, with a probe container on this box's docker 29.4.1: one SIGTERM to the front end left it running, the real `supervisor.stop` stopped it in 10.2 s). A container record is stopped through the daemon by its recorded 64-hex id (`docker stop --time 10`), never by name. The record is kept while the daemon cannot confirm it is gone, and no launcher ever gets SIGKILL. Process records (vLLM) keep the SIGTERM path unchanged. - **T-0132, live default 1120 + ReGround, pass-2 crop budget, 3-arm A/B harness.** Merged 2026-10-22 at b3f5613 (verified PASS-with-notes at 369f489; two reviewers per group).
  - Live mode now defaults to `visual_tokens=1120` and ReGround on (human ruling, overriding T-0131 D5 "no change"; D5's 1.5 s gate recorded as mis-calibrated). A `grounding:` line and turn-line key say each value's source.

- **T-0131, live grounding: image budget, ReGround, verify-and-scroll guidance, 6-arm A/B harness.** Code merged 2026-10-22. Every group was reviewed and approved, by two reviewers for B, and verified PASS-with-notes at 19ecde6.
  - **`--visual-tokens 280|560|1120`** sets `mm_processor_kwargs.max_soft_tokens` on every request. With it unset, the request is byte-identical to before. The engine default is 280, so screens previously went in at 280, not the 1120 the policy file claimed.
  - **`--reground`** (live only) sends a second, never-voiced request on a 0.4 crop around the first click and keeps only its x/y. Freshness is re-checked after it, and a cancel race found in review (F1) is fixed.
  - **Two live-only prompt sentences:** check the post-action screenshot before claiming success, and scroll before answering about a whole page.
  - **Next:** the GPU A/B, `scripts/locked/t0131_grounding_ab.sh` via `/launch`. It runs 150 turns over budget {280, 560, 1120} × ReGround {off, on}, and applies the pre-registered decision rule (H-T131-1).
- **T-0111g, `tools = live`: computer use in the agent's own browser** (merged 2026-10-21; groups A, B and C each reviewed by two reviewers, or one for C, with several rounds; verified PASS-with-notes at 0c5e6e6).
  - **Group A.** Executor options (headed, end on window close, origin allow-list), the loopback start-page server (`local_astra/pages/`), and three demo pages (shop, contact, deals).
    - In live, the fence narrows to the start page's own origin, so a page cannot reach vLLM, STT, TTS or the HUD on loopback.
    - Redirects are blocked and SharedWorker is removed.
  - **Group B.** The session wiring:
    - the agent browser's own frames under capture identity;
    - the post-action image swapped into each step;
    - stale-frame refusal;
    - honest failed results, and the window-closed notice;
    - `--tools live` / `--browser` refusals, doctor's browser rows and the docs.
  - **Group C.** The GPU smoke harness and locked script: 5 tasks × 5 turns, with report-only metrics and safety counts. CPU dress rehearsals cover two real turns, plus a planted prefill leak that the sentinel scan caught.
  - **Next:** the human types `/launch` for C-G1 (stop the STT container first). The desk try happens if 25/25 turns are accepted and every safety count is 0. Then real websites (H-T111c-E) and T-0128c.
- **T-0128, HUD redesign part 1** (merged 2026-10-21; the reviewer approved after two rounds and the verifier passed it with notes at b1f5cc0). The approved mockup look:
  - a header with the turn pill and a System/Light/Dark setting (default System, stored per browser);
  - the conversation column on the left and an always-on right rail: what I see, Thinking with an honest empty denoising slot, assists, log, diagnostics.
  - Token values were moved until WCAG floors hold in both themes; the mockup's faint text and status colours failed.
  - The screenshots (`scripts/demo/hud_screens.py`) show the T-0127-repaired shopping-list card themed in both modes.
  - Next: T-0128b (inline cards), T-0128c (the real denoising view).
- **T-0111c, browser executor core** (merged 2026-10-21; the reviewer approved after four rounds and the verifier passed it with notes at 8dabbaf). `local_astra/computer/` is a `ToolExecutor` that drives Playwright 1.64.0 with Chromium 156.0.8078.4, in an ephemeral context at 1280×800, DSF 1.
  - It runs the v1 actions: click_at, type_at, scroll and press_key, with search staying dry-run.
  - The loopback egress fence is the resolver rule, a route allowlist, a WebSocket route and blocked service workers. WebRTC is removed by an init script plus a UDP policy flag. Zero egress was measured on 23 vectors and 11 WebRTC bypasses.
  - Frame identity:
    - a DCT pHash salted per browser generation, with retired phashes refused after a restart;
    - `page_serial`, so a popup adopted in `ensure()` refuses the pending action, and a frame taken on another page is refused;
    - one 8 s action timeout covering launch, adoption and dispatch;
    - a cancelled launch is never used unfenced.
  - Not wired into the session yet; that is T-0111g (`--tools live`). Tests: `make browser`, 28 rows.
- **T-0127, Card children repair and incomplete-card reporting** (merged 2026-10-20; reviewers A (code, two rounds) and B (data) approved, verified PASS-with-notes at a04252a). The 2026-10-20 desk cards rendered empty because the model writes Card `children` (A2UI wants one `child`); the gate pruned the Card, emptied its parent and still reported valid. Now: CC repair (one id → `child`, several → a `__gate_<id>_body` Column), `GateResult.complete/lost`, `ui_complete_final` on the turn line, and a restore fix (a re-sent id's references restore from this canvas's copy). Desk log replay 0/6 → 5/6 complete (t3 lost content as ruled). Recorded GPU trials, strict-complete per 160 Q12 positives: 6720e56 A0 122→135, C3 92→100; e6c3a81 A0 116→133, K1 120→130, K2 101→109; 0 regressions. Gate p95 15.9–18.5 ms on a loaded box (bar 17.6, HEAD/BASE ≈ 1.00); re-measure idle. Follow-ups: T-0127d (t7 duplicate id + self-reference; pin reviewer A's parent-first-on-k row; `replay_ui_gate.py --help` treats the flag as a path), T-0127b (prompt Card `child` example, GPU A/B).
- **T-0107e, honour a clear no** (merged 2026-10-20 at 411d217; reviewers A and B approved, verified). When the user's turn is a clear no, stop, cancel or don't (`user_declines`, a closed lexicon on the first clause, with reversal words such as "wait" and "instead" cancelling it), every call drafted in that turn is refused (`the user said no`), at every loop step, before any repair. Nothing is held across turns and the machine is untouched. Re-read of recorded GPU trials: the 62/62 T-0107b actions taken after "No, leave it." are refused, and 0 of the other 508 routed T-0107b trials or 3,570 earlier routed trials. Serial 310 s, parallel 44.8 s. Known gaps: "leave it", "never mind" and "Don't send the message, I'll do it" don't count, and neither does a "no, stop" spoken while the model is still THINKING (it joins the request's words); the model's own "I will …" is still voiced before a refused call.
- **T-0107d, glued question asks** (merged 2026-10-19 at 8059dcd; reviewer approved, verified). A `?` is a non-question only when it is glued to a lowercase letter or a digit, so `Shall I pay?Then` now asks while `3?4` and `pass?word` stay false. Re-read of the 3,570 trials: 0 flagged (independently reproduced by the reviewer). Serial 310 s, parallel 44.6 s. Noted, not fixed: no row pins a `?` glued to an islower-but-not-alnum symbol such as ⓐ (it errs toward refusing), and some test docstrings still say T-0107a2.
- **T-0107a, ask-first backstop** (merged 2026-10-19 at 3d27c2f; reviewers A and B, a re-review of the replaced predicate, verified). The human ruled for an autonomous agent: it asks only before rare high-stakes actions it judges to be a payment/order, a send, or a deletion. If a step's voiced text asks a question (`asks_user`: a `?` not glued to an alnum and not followed by a lowercase word; linear), the call drafted in that step is refused (`asked the user first`) and the turn ends with the question. Nothing is held across turns, and there is no machine, contract or HUD change. Re-read of 3,570 earlier GPU tool trials: 0 flagged. Serial 303.2 s, parallel 44.2 s. Docs: docs/01 §3 status, docs/02 §5, docs/03 Q13 delta (the last written by the main session at the human's instruction). Follow-up **T-0107d**: a `?` glued to an uppercase word.
- **T-0126, end-of-turn literals kept out of history with tools on** (merged 2026-10-19 at 34aec39; reviewers A and B, verified). The tools-on cleaner cuts at `<end_of_turn>`/`<|endoftext|>` after T-0117's cleaning, so history, `astra.spoken`, `turns.jsonl`, memory, `spoken_prefix` and the repair seed hold only what was voiced. The voice is unchanged, I4 is unchanged (`Press  now. `), and there is one count-only note per stream. Re-scopes R1–R3; the criterion-11 rows compare the fixed range 4a25a85..b3dc62b. Serial 302.5 s and parallel 47.5 s (n=1, idle).
- **T-0110c, HUD static server poll seam** (merged 2026-10-18 at 2da2c5b; reviewer approved, verified on an idle box). `HudStaticServer(..., *, poll_interval=0.5)`; tests pass 0.01, production unchanged (`cli.py` untouched, effective 0.5 s checked). `test_hud_static.py` 6.6 → 0.78 s. Serial median 292.9 s (n=3, load < 1.3; bar 297 s met, T-0110's 280 s still open), `make fast-parallel` gated median 44.0 s (43.6–46.4 s, thin against 45 s). Next for the serial bar: T-0110d (audio waits, ≈ 53 s, high blast) and T-0110e (STT quiet windows, ≈ 20 s); the 120 s ceiling needs CPU-bound rows to shrink too (H-T110c-4, with the human).
- **T-0124, special literals kept out of history with tools off** (merged 2026-10-18 at 2580ddd; reviewers A and B, verified). A second cleaner stage after T-0121's applies the speech gate's own cut/drop rule before the machine sees the text, so history, `astra.spoken`, `turns.jsonl` and memory hold only what was voiced; the `<eos>`-spliced thought (`Sure, <|chan<eos>nel>thought SECRET…`) no longer reaches history or the HUD. Voice unchanged; one count-only note per stream. Re-scopes R1–R3 named in the task. Follow-up **T-0126**: with tools on, `<end_of_turn>`/`<|endoftext|>` (gate trim literals, not tokenizer literals) still reach history; rows pinning that are marked for T-0126. Serial 316.8 s (load 1.3–2.2), parallel 44.9 s.
- **T-0110b, faster fast suite** (merged 2026-10-18; reviewers A and B approved, verified on an idle box). The session rig's fakes no longer sleep on wall time. `make fast-parallel` median 44.18 s (was ~57–64 s; bar 45 s met); serial 303 s (was ~400 s; the 280 s bar stays open as T-0110c, human ruling). New guard rows keep real waits from creeping back; the T-0117/T-0121 unowned-files rows now check closed ranges.
- **T-0120b, compaction wired into the tool loop** (merged 2026-10-18; reviewers A and B approved, verified). Before each step request the session compacts older tool responses (the last 2 kept, only when the next continuation would exceed `MAX_CONTINUATION`), keeps the compacted steps so the prefix cache holds, logs a `tool loop compacted` note and counts `tool_compactions` on the turn line. Elided search results read "earlier result omitted; search again if you need it" (human, 2026-10-18); action stubs keep "earlier result omitted". Only a loop that still does not fit ends `over_budget`. T-0120c (a small GPU re-check that the model now re-searches) is proposed, not started.
- **T-0123, tool loop on by default whenever tools are on** (merged 2026-10-18; human ruling; reviewers A and B approved, verified). `--tools dry-run` now runs the multi-step loop (8 steps) unless `--no-tool-loop` or `tool_loop = false`; tools stay off by default, and that default session is byte-identical. Tools-on runs carry `:loop8` in their regime label (`--no-tool-loop` restores the old label).
- **T-0121, thought text never voiced or stored with tools off** (merged 2026-10-18; reviewers A and B approved, verified). A thought-only cleaner on the default (tools-off) path removes closed thought blocks and cuts at an unclosed one before the speech gate, so history equals what was played; one note counts the dropped characters. Without a channel marker, output is byte-identical. Follow-up **T-0124**: other special literals (`<eos>`, `<turn|>`, `<|tool_call>`) trimmed from the voice still reach history with tools off, including a thought marker spliced by `<eos>` (confirmed leak into history and the HUD).
- **T-0115 / T-0115a, multi-step tool-loop GPU probe** (run 2026-10-18 at c398fdf; write-ups merged 1ca1689). T-0115 `ready` on every pre-registered bar; T-0115a E `inconclusive` (spelled-out numbers missing from the accepted list); see *Runs*.
- **T-0117, thought text in an answer commit is never voiced or stored** (merged 2026-10-17; reviewers A and B approved, verified). With tools on, the answer cleaner removes closed thought blocks (and their words) and cuts at an unclosed one before anything is spoken, stored or sent as a repair seed; a session-log note counts the dropped characters, never the text. Without a marker, output is byte-identical to before. Follow-ups: T-0121 (the same leak on the tools-off path, confirmed at session level: history stores the thought), T-0122 (spliced thought text can reach the next loop step's request).
- **T-0120, tool-loop compaction helper** (merged 2026-10-17; reviewer approved, verified). Pure `loop.compact(steps, budget)`: when a loop's continuation would exceed its budget, older tool responses (never the last 2) become a stub `{note:"earlier result omitted", results:N}` until it fits 2/3 of the budget (human ruling H-T115-0). Not wired into the session yet (T-0120b, after the T-0115 probe).
- **T-0114, tool-result loop session wiring** (merged 2026-10-17; reviewers A and B approved, verified). `astra session --tools dry-run --tool-loop [--max-tool-steps N]` runs multi-step tool turns: execute in a task with a 10 s session-clock deadline, render the result with form G, continue with `continue_final_message`, judge each step, speak only at the start/end/when stuck (D16). Off by default; loop-off output byte-identical to d3bc6ce. No search backend is wired yet (T-0111b). Next: T-0115 GPU probe (`/launch`), T-0114b HUD step label, follow-ups T-0117–T-0119 (see the task file).
- **T-0110 parts A and C, fast suite** (merged 2026-10-17; reviewer approved, verified). A per-harness memo of the card reading and a 0.01 s stub shutdown poll: `make fast-parallel` 62.7 → 52.45 s median (n=3, load 1.6–4.8), serial ~474 → 358 s (n=1, loaded). No check removed. Part B (zero-wait session rig) is parked unreviewed as T-0110b at 0a190aa after one unidentified failure in 28 runs; the 45 s / 280 s bars wait on it.
- **T-0112, tool-result loop in the turn machine** (merged 2026-10-17 at c345038; reviewers A and B approved, verified). Rows R11–R18 behind `TurnPolicy.tool_loop` (off by default): execute → await `tool.result` → `llm.continue` up to `max_tool_steps` (8), routine steps silent (D16), history joined across playback runs, a takeover after an action never re-runs it. Loop off is identical to the frozen T-0089 machine (L8 differential). Session wiring is T-0114.
- **T-0108b, card component repairs** (merged 2026-10-17 at ca47b51; reviewers A and B approved, verified). The UI gate repairs schema-invalid A2UI components without inventing text: TextField `placeholder`→`label`, DateTimeInput `value: ""`, ChoicePicker string options → objects with `value: []`, Button `children`→`child`, and inline children lifted. Text-only Buttons get a reserved `__gate_<id>_label` Text and a `__gate.clicked` action; a click only sends the label back to the model (human ruling: cards are illustrations). A stale-stub bug that lost whole cards is fixed.
  - CPU re-score of the stored runs (complete cards of 160): T-0108a A0 54→132, K1 64→132, K2 66→108; T-0103 A0 63→136, C3 32→101.
  - Flight forms: A0 1→31 and 0→33 (e6c3a81); 0→27 and 0→37 (6720e56).
  - Gate p95 is 16 ms.
  - Follow-ons: a pre-registered K1-under-new-gate `/launch` (H-T108b (e), queued for the human); T-0108e (resample).
- **T-0113 tool-result rendering** (merged 2026-10-17 at 8035bd6; reviewed, verified). The first piece of the T-0111 loop: `local_astra/toolcall/loop.py`, byte-identical to the pinned Gemma 4 template (cd oracle), with special-literal sanitising to a fixpoint (keys included), bounds of 5 results × 300 chars and 1500 per response, `step_prompt` (form G), and the `SearchBackend` protocol plus the search contracts. T-0111a sub-tasks are renumbered T-0112 to T-0115. Notes for T-0114 are in T-0111a.md: pass thought-stripped step text, and bound call args.
- **T-0108 GenUI structural repair** (merged 2026-10-16 at aa333f2; reviewers A and B approved, verified). The diagnosis: most rejected A2UI cards are model-emitted bracket corruption, a duplicated `{"` and wrong closers, confirmed in the token ids. The UI gate now runs a linear, string-aware bracket repair that edits only structural characters, and only after strict parsing fails. Grounding is still against the original text, and the gate no longer raises on deep nesting. The turn line gains `ui_valid_final` and `ui_shown`; `ui_valid` is unchanged. CPU re-score of stored runs (n = 1 per trial, deterministic):
  - T-0103 A0: last card OK 103 → 149/153; failed calls 126 → 36/376; shown 129 → 153/160.
  - T-0103 C3: last card OK 80 → 114/128.
  - T-0098c pooled: last card OK 58 → 83/88.
  - Repair p95 0.09 ms; gate p95 14.6 ms.

  Next:
  - T-0109, the seeded retry after a shown partial (H-T108a);
  - T-0108a, the prompt teaching interactive components' required fields (many valid cards still lose TextFields without `label`);
  - T-0108b, executing the resample;
  - T-0108c, json_repair's quadratic cost.
- **T-0104 speaking policy** (merged 2026-10-16 at 5dc2e1e; reviewed, verified): docs/01 D16. The assistant speaks at the start, before anything irreversible (and confirms, even when asked explicitly), when stuck, and at the end. Routine steps are not narrated. `live` requires the confirmation gate (T-0107). The spoken-before-every-call targets are superseded (nine dated deltas), and docs/03 Q13 holds the definitions only. Follow-ons: T-0105 (Q13 cases and CPU re-score), T-0107 (confirmation gate), then T-0106 (GPU baseline and A/B). Next focus by the human: GenUI reliability.
- **T-0102 refuse retracted calls** (merged 2026-10-16 at 2730ce4; reviewers A and B approved, verified): a native draft that is not valid and has more model text after its call (letters or digits once special literals are removed) is refused, `"model text after call"`, with no repair and no execution. Clean cuts, special-only tails and second calls are unchanged; T-0100's reasons take precedence (H-T101c; Q1 closed calls too). Across 3062 stored commits exactly one verdict flips: the df379e4 kout-03 Ctrl+Shift+T retraction. Serial fast suite ~350 s on main and branch alike (the ceiling is 120 s; a standing issue, not T-0102).
- **T-0051 group B the held word** (tests dispatched 2026-09-16): group A fixed which clock the backstop measures; it does **not** release a word the recogniser never closed. At the desk the human's last word was recognised and then held inside `MoshiSttClient` for **21 040 ms**, waiting for an `EndWord` that never came because the audio never stopped (1372 gated blocks = exactly 13.72 s played), and was finally released as a 21-second-long word. B adds `end_estimated` to `SttWordEvent`, a bounded hold (recommended 1000 ms, ≈ 2× the measured 510 ms delivery delay; per-word lags measured 874/875/890/923 ms in session time), the per-word delivery-lag statistics, and criterion 12 — a word published by the hold may never *open* a run, or the fix would create an accidental barge-in through the STT tap (SPEAKING → PAUSED), which T-0032's design ruling and T-0034 forbid. Unblocked by lending `stt_client.py` from T-0048 group A (a6210f9): T-0048 group A is not dispatched while B is in flight and the file returns when B merges. **Implemented and reviewed twice; the suite is green at 2270 passed.** Both reviewers accepted the implementation against criteria 8-12 and 16. What came back was test gaps and one withdrawn ruling of mine (B36): the boundary-tie change I ordered is **provably inert** — the audio clock moves only in `feed()`, which re-checks on the very next line with no `await`, so the other release site fired **0 times in 206 tests** while the feed side fired twice. The only build honouring it makes the release message-driven, which holds a word forever once the messages stop — the original 21-second failure, and exactly what T-0048's feed gate does. Follow-ups in flight: the inert site and its false comment removed (B37), a counter split so it stops meaning both "the server is ahead of us" and "we gave up too early" (B38, bounded by B40), and five owed rows for rulings written after the tests (B32, B33). The test-author found the session-level reason this was never caught (**ruling B19**): `tests/fakes/stt.py` stamped its step events with the *model's* audio position while `MoshiSttClient` uses the *fed-audio* clock, so over the fake a word's end always equalled "now" and the endpointer observed a delivery lag of **zero however large the configured lag was**. The double could not express the condition, so no session-level row could ever have seen the defect that cut the human off. Inherited as a hard constraint on T-0048, whose feed gate changes what the fed clock means. Three criteria were amended as unsatisfiable or wrong as written (B22-B24) and two open questions decided (B25).
- **T-0051 group A the endpointer's clock** (merged 2026-09-16; two reviewers, revised, verified): the silence backstop subtracted
  two clocks — *now* from the fed-audio clock, *the last word's end* from when it was spoken — so 800 ms of nominal silence was about
  **290 ms the recogniser had actually examined**, and any pause over a third of a second ended the turn. It now measures from when
  a word **arrived**, in one clock, exact for any reporting delay. Two guards stop a late word re-opening a closed run, each with a
  discriminating row: absent, the turn oscillates; **too wide, the endpointer goes deaf for seconds after everything you say**, and
  that second direction was unpinned until a review caught it with all 37 rows green.
  Diagnosed entirely from the human's own session log. **It does not restore their missing word** — that is group B's bounded hold.

- **T-0043 the session log and its checkbox** (groups A and B merged 2026-09-16; each reviewed, revised and verified): a JSONL file
  per session, flushed per line, images stripped structurally, with taps at the machine, the model, the UI gate, speech, the page
  wire and the turn record — plus a checkbox that shows **the server's** state and never the click, says it is waiting on an
  unechoed click, and shows **unknown rather than on** before anything arrives. The review extended that to the server: a log that
  disables itself on a write error now announces it, so a page that stays connected cannot keep a ticked box over a session
  recording nothing. **It has already paid for itself**: the human's next session produced a 12451-record log from two spoken
  sentences, and five findings came out of it including the endpointer clock bug that became T-0051.
  **Owed:** the live browser check (group C's tool), the opening record's payload (criterion 17, partly unmet, in the status line),
  and **a regression test for the cancellation race whose fix is code-reviewed only** (ruling B27).

- **T-0049 `astra up` tells the truth about what it started** (groups A and B merged 2026-09-16; each reviewed, revised and
  independently verified): a pure three-way decision, the launcher's exit status actually read, newness proved against a reference
  taken before the spawn, a failed container query treated as **unknown** rather than absent, the state record written only after
  verification, one row per service, and `--restart` as the only thing that signals anything. Origin: the human's `astra up` printed
  started and ok for all three services while two of three had failed. **Unblocks T-0048 group B**, whose bench would otherwise have
  measured the old engine while reporting the new number. Group C, the live cycle, is a `/launch` the human invokes.
  Two consequences to expect rather than mistake for regressions: a cold start is now **slower by design**, because each service is
  confirmed healthy before the next begins; and start failures now print to stdout where the old verb used stderr.

- **T-0045 the session opens the microphone it names** (merged 2026-09-16; reviewed REVISE, fixed, verified): `--mic <node>`,
  `--no-mic`, and with neither the box's default capture source read from the graph rather than guessed, named on the startup and
  regime lines, with a loud refusal when the node does not exist or none can be found. Origin: the human spoke at the desk and
  nothing appeared, because the CLI opened the device with a sink and no capture source. **The review found a second lie:**
  `ASTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE` — the workaround the human was told to export — was read two layers down, so `--no-mic` printed `mic=none`
  while a stream opened on it. It is now a printed step at a ruled precedence and the off path defeats it. **Owed:** the live
  check of a human speaking (`/launch` + the rig), and seven rows in two other tasks' modules that inherit the same variable.

- **T-0049 group A `astra up` tells the truth about what it started** (merged 2026-09-16; reviewed REVISE, fixed, verified):
  a pure three-way decision (start / skip / refuse), the launcher's exit status actually read, newness proved against a reference
  taken before the spawn, a failed container query treated as **unknown** rather than absent, and the state record written only
  after verification. Origin: the human's `astra up` printed started and ok for all three services while two of three had failed.
  **What the review added:** the first implementation still reported a two-hour-old container as started and wrote its foreign id
  to disk whenever the daemon could not be asked — permission-denied being the ordinary state on this box. **Unblocks T-0048
  group B**, whose whole purpose is to reboot the engine and measure it. Groups B (the verbs, `--restart`) and C (the live cycle,
  a `/launch`) are next; B is blocked on `cli.py` behind T-0045.


- **T-0047 group A0 the listening meter's level and room-noise floor** (merged 2026-09-16; reviewed, escalated, fixed, verified):
  `local_astra/reflex/level.py` — a pure peak-held RMS with a running P20 noise floor over 3 s, a `voiced` verdict relative to the
  room, and a 100 ms throttle. Origin: the human had nothing to look at for 20 s at the desk. **The review's escalation was the
  real find:** the capture path substitutes exact zeros for self-speech-gated blocks, and eight of them dragged the floor to −100,
  so an unchanged room read as a voiced full bar for **2320 ms after every assistant utterance** — worse than the frozen bar the
  feature exists to prevent. Frames at or below the silence constant no longer enter the floor window (ruling A7).
  **Named input to group B (ruling A18, measured):** the floor is now frozen for the whole gated stretch, so if the room genuinely
  changes while the assistant talks, the page can show a false `voiced` for up to ~3 s once listening resumes, and criterion 15's
  stale-guard does not cover it because real frames keep arriving. The page must not ship the meter without either an
  `SttFrame.gated` flag or an explicit decision to accept that artefact. Groups A and B stay blocked on T-0043 group B.
- **T-0044 the prompt teaches the A2UI channel** (group A merged 2026-09-16; reviewed, fixed, verified): the system prefix now ends with a compact A2UI block — the exact `<a2ui>` fence, one worked example the gate's own validator accepts, the 15 basic components the bundle can render, and the four rules of the shown channel. Origin: the model narrated a surface instead of emitting one on every desk turn (`ui_valid: null`). The review caught two vacuous rule pins (the spelling `json` was satisfied by the example's own `catalog.json`, `aloud` by "say the answer aloud as usual"), fixed and mutation-proven; the verifier re-derived every mutation independently. **Owed, both needing /launch:** criterion 5b (the block's token count as the engine's `/tokenize` counts it — 1779 code points is a structural bound, not a token claim) and criterion 9 (the live eval, 3 show-me requests plus 1 plain question, n=3). Structural risk still open: a fence spanning two canvases is split by the client (B7/M7, ruling A7) — a minimal 5-row surface measures 1060 fenced characters, over one 256-token canvas, so the straddle case is expected to fire.
- **T-0042 audio smoke never finishes** (2026-09-16, merged): the drain wait polls `queued_ms()` with a ceiling instead of writing silence into the queue it waits on; rows over a real-time-paced stub driver; live on the null-sink rig 11/11 (peak 0.25, peers verified). Owed: the underflow snapshot timing and the failed-run label.
- **T-0041 `astra up` after a reboot** (2026-09-15, merged): `services/tts.sh` starts Pocket TTS through `uvx --from pocket-tts==3.1.0` (the lock's pin, human-written) on the pinned cores; the engine's probe budget is 240 s (a cold start measured ≈ 130 s); a FAILED row quotes the service log's last line. - 2026-09-16 22:17 **T-0048 ruling A13 cold-engine test — RUN, and it FAILED LOUDLY, which is the result.** `bash scripts/locked/t0048_cold_boot.sh` @ **73a85dd** (clean tree; all four pre-flight refusals passed). The engine **cannot cold-boot while the recogniser is resident**, and it does not get as far as allocating anything:
  ```
  ValueError: Free memory on device cuda:0 (27.23/31.33 GiB) on startup is less than desired
  GPU memory utilization (0.92, 28.83 GiB). Decrease GPU memory utilization or reduce GPU
  memory used by other processes.
  ```
  **Validity gate satisfied:** 27.23 GiB free at startup, not the ~30.4 GiB of an empty device, so the recogniser really was resident (3 180 MiB, container up 5 h; Chrome 50 MiB). Died **21 s after launch**, at `gpu_worker.py:440 → utils.py:526`, *before* weight load, KV reservation or graph capture — so `Initial free memory`, `GPU KV cache size` and the sampler transient were never reached and **remain unmeasured**. `Resolved architecture: AstraDiffusionGemma` did appear, so the plugin path was live. VRAM trace `logs/vram-coldboot-73a85dd.csv` (390 samples at 200 ms) shows a peak of 4 205 MiB used / 27 882 MiB free — the container starting and exiting, nothing more. Log `logs/vllm-coldboot-73a85dd.log` (155 lines).

  **The mechanism is not the allocator arena; it is a startup pre-check nobody had hit.** vLLM demands `gpu_memory_utilization` of the **whole device** be free *at startup* — 0.92 × 31.33 = 28.83 GiB — and `services/vllm.sh` **never passes `--gpu-memory-utilization`**, so the 0.92 default applies. With the recogniser holding 3.18 GiB only 27.23 GiB is free, which is 1.60 GiB short. **This has nothing to do with the KV pool**, which is carved out of that arena later.

  **Why nobody saw it before:** the warm engine that ran 23 h was started when the GPU was **empty** (30.43 GiB free, comfortably over 28.83), and the recogniser moved to the GPU afterwards. It has been running on conditions that can no longer be reproduced. **Every restart in this configuration fails**, which makes this a release blocker rather than a curiosity, and it is exactly what the test was for.

  **The workaround, and it is just the boot order.** The engine needs 28.83 GiB free *at startup* but settles at about 28.4 GiB used, so **start the engine first and the recogniser second** — which is precisely the accidental order that produced the working 23-hour setup. Starting them the other way round can never work at 0.92. Until the flag lands, a usable box means: stop `astra-stt`, boot the engine, then start the recogniser. `astra up` **already does this** — correction from T-0058's planner, verified against `cli.py:104`: the engine is first in its plan and, after T-0049, each start waits for health before the next begins; my earlier claim that it did not know was wrong, and the failing order only arises when services are started by hand. **Second correction, same source: "~0.85 with margin" was wrong in the direction that matters.** The demand must be computed against vLLM's own denominator (32 082 MiB, not `nvidia-smi`'s 32 607; the two free readings agree exactly at 27 882). At 0.86 the check demands 27 591 MiB — 291 below the observed free, so it boots, and 157 **above** the engine's real footprint of ≈ 27 434, so it still refuses a configuration the engine could not survive. At 0.85 it demands 27 270, which is 164 MiB **below** what the engine actually uses: margin on the check, not on the physics, so it would pass boots that then die later and deeper. **0.86 is the value**; the arithmetic with provenance is in `docs/tasks/T-0058.md`.

  **Consequences.** (i) The fix is one flag on `services/vllm.sh` — `--gpu-memory-utilization` at most 27.23/31.33 = **0.869**, so ~0.85 with margin — which is T-0048 group B's file and needs its own review. (ii) Ruling A13's question ("can the allocator grow its arena from the driver rather than recycle a warm one?") is **still unanswered**: the run never reached the allocator. (iii) The sampler transient is still unmeasured, so the 2.5 GiB floor remains ten assumed copies of a 0.25 GiB tensor. (iv) `astra doctor` should refuse this configuration before a boot burns 21 s — there is no row for it, which is the same gap T-0048 criterion 19 names for the lock mismatch.

## Decisions taken (2026-09-12)
- Conductor design derived from Unmute's `unmute_handler.py` (MIT), no code copied; our pure turn machine replaces its logic as we iterate on turn policy
- Memory-lite for v1: SQLite + FTS5, (ts, jpeg, title) on scene change, DiffusionGemma writes caption+OCR when idle, top-3 JPEGs attached
- Plain web page HUD (not Tauri); L1 pause-then-cancel barge-in + acknowledgement clips only for v1; constrained-decoding and dual-branch subprojects start after the demo video (X6 still runs in Phase 0)
- Perception is sequenced: browser tab (`getDisplayMedia`/`getUserMedia` → JPEG at 1 fps over the WebSocket) for v1; native portal/PipeWire daemon in M2+ behind the same `PerceptionClient`
- Thinking always on (D11), confirmed; X7 measures thought length on easy questions and the closing-tag rate
- `talker` profile: Gemma 4 E2B (llama.cpp Q4, GPU) as front-of-house model, never asserting content; dual-branch patch parked; X9 decides E2B-hears vs Nemotron CPU STT
- Single-canvas drill mode on DiffusionGemma alone (docs/11 §7); first solve cached in TutorState
- Generative UI: A2UI v0.9.1 + `edu` catalog, validate-and-repair (docs/12 §1); denoising view from the plugin tap (docs/12 §2); pixel-first perception with optional assists (docs/12 §3) — decided 2026-09-12

## Arrival checklist (written 2026-09-14 for the human's first physical session; do these in order)

Main is green at the commit named in `git log` (fast suite ≈ 1800 passed parallel; ruff/mypy clean). Everything below is either a
one-line install, a read-only check, or a `/launch`. Nothing here has been run on hardware yet — the audio device has never met PortAudio.

0. ~~done 2026-09-15 (works with the shared screen)~~ **Text mode first (no audio needed; T-0039 merged and proven live on 2026-09-15):** `uv run astra session --silent --serve-hud`, open the printed URL (`http://127.0.0.1:1420/?ws=ws://127.0.0.1:8000/v1/realtime`), type a question in the box, read the answer in the panes. Nothing is played aloud. Without a browser: `uv run python scripts/demo/text_mode_probe.py "your question"` prints the turn over the wire. Ctrl-C exits cleanly (T-0040).
1. ~~P1~~ **done 2026-09-15** (`libportaudio2` installed; 13 PortAudio devices). Services started; see T-0041 for the two startup defects. ~~**P1 — `sudo apt-get install -y libportaudio2`**~~, then `uv run python -c "import sounddevice as sd; print(sd.query_devices())"`. Expect
   JACK/ALSA stderr spew before the list (harmless; `JACK_NO_START_SERVER=1` quiets it).
2. **A real sink.** Only `auto_null` exists; the analog profiles are all `available: no`. Either plug a headset/DP-HDMI, or
   `wpctl set-profile 40 2`, then `wpctl status` must list a non-Dummy sink. Skip this for the null-sink rig step, not for anything acoustic.
3. **`uv run astra doctor`** (T-0033; read-only, no GPU). PASS/WARN/FAIL per line with the fix. Expected after 1–2: `audio.*` PASS,
   `container.stt_device` WARN (CPU STT, RTF ≈ 1.3 — your decision, see below), `lock.pins` WARN (pocket_tts/chat_template TBD).
4. **Audio first contact on the null-sink rig** (no speaker needed): `bash scripts/audio_rig.sh up` then
   `uv run python scripts/demo/audio_smoke.py --sink astra-test-sink --source astra-test-sink.monitor`. Exit 0 with a peak well above
   0.16; `peak == 0` ⇒ target absent, `0.11–0.16` ⇒ you captured the webcam. Read the `graph quantum (pw-top)` line — the first live quantum.
5. **Speaking to it** (T-0045, in review 2026-09-16 — do this step only once it has merged). `uv run astra session --serve-hud` opens
   the box's default capture source and names it on the startup and regime lines; `--mic <node>` picks another, `--no-mic` opens none.
   Add `--sink astra-test-sink` to keep it silent. **Unset `ASTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE` first** — the 2026-09-15 workaround is being turned into a
   printed precedence step (ruling A14), and until that lands the variable can open a microphone a run says it did not open. Speech clients
   are already proven live (Runs: 7/7 contract rows, census 3/3 — the recogniser echoes its marker 3.0 s after the mark on CPU); expect the
   CPU lag until T-0048 lands.
6. **The listening rung** (T-0032 group B, `/launch`): `uv run python scripts/demo/session_rung.py --listen --expect "…" --expect "…"
   --expect "…" --run-dir logs/rung2-<ts>` — state the three sentences before speaking them; then
   `ASTRA_LISTEN_RUN=logs/rung2-<ts> uv run pytest tests/integration/test_session_listening_live.py -q -m "gpu or audio"`. The log prints

## Live finding 2026-09-16: `astra up` reports success on a start that failed

The human ran `astra up --profile lean` against an already-running box. It printed `started` for all
three services and then `ok` for all three. **Two of the three starts had failed.** Verified on the
box minutes later:

- the three pids `up` recorded: vllm `3574540` **dead**, stt `3574545` **dead**, tts `3574555` alive;
- `logs/stt.log`: `Conflict. The container name "/astra-stt" is already in use by container 61f0d405…`;
- `docker ps`: the containers actually serving are the **originals**, `Up 2 hours`;
- so the health probe passed against the old containers, which is why nothing looked wrong.

Mechanism: `cli.up` (local_astra/cli.py:139-152) runs every command in the start plan unconditionally
— no health check first, no idempotence — and the detached runner never sees the launcher's non-zero
exit, so the `isinstance(handle, int)` guard at line 148 cannot fire. The pid files are then
overwritten with dead launcher pids, which is also why `astra status` says `stt … not running (stale
pid file)` for a service that is healthy: for the containerised services the recorded pid is the
launcher (`services/stt.sh` re-execs under `sg docker -c`), which exits while its container lives on.

**Why this blocks T-0048 group B.** That task's entire purpose is to reboot the engine with a smaller
KV pool and measure it. If `up` silently no-ops against a running container, the bench measures the
**old 4 GiB engine** while the report claims 2.5 GiB — an unattributable number of exactly the kind
CLAUDE.md forbids, and one that would look like a success. Fix this before the engine group runs.

**Confirmed working in the same run:** T-0041's speech-service fix. `services/tts.sh` now starts
Pocket TTS 3.1.0 through `uvx --from`, pinned, on cores 2,3, and it is the one service with a live
supervised pid. The `taskset: failed to execute pocket-tts` line in the first status was the stale log
of the pre-T-0041 attempt, not a new failure.

- **T-0049 `astra up` tells the truth about what it started** (planned 2026-09-16; A medium, B **high/two reviewers**, C a /launch
  run): three defects compose into the lie — every command runs unconditionally, the launcher's non-zero exit is dropped on the
  floor, and a health probe answers for *any* instance on the port, so a stale one certifies a start that never happened. After it,
  a start reports success only when this invocation produced a **new**, healthy instance whose identity it can name; an
  already-running service is skipped out loud; a service running from a **different script than the one on disk** is a refusal, not
  a skip (that is T-0048 group B's case, caught for free); and `--restart` is the only way `up` signals anything. **Blocks T-0048
  group B.** Group A is startable now; group B queues behind T-0043 group A on `cli.py`.

## What the first logged session bought (2026-09-16 desk run, 12451 records)

The human spoke two sentences. The log turned that into five findings nobody could have reached by
reading code, and it is the single strongest argument for the logging feature they asked for.

- **The endpointer cuts people off, and the mechanism is a two-clock subtraction** (T-0051): it measures
  "now" in the fed-audio clock and "the last word ended" in the clock of when it was spoken, and the
  difference is always the recogniser's reporting delay. So 800 ms of nominal silence was **~290 ms the
  recogniser had actually examined**. The pause heads were working and would have decided ~160 ms later.
- **The stranded word was held inside the client**, not starved of audio as the orchestrator first said:
  1372 gated blocks is exactly the 13.72 s the assistant spoke, and the 80 ms step stream has no gap in
  787 s. It waited for an end marker that never came and was released by the *next* word's arrival.
- **The device half of T-0048 is now evidenced**: end-of-turn echo **473–482 ms** on the GPU against
  **3034 ms** on the CPU, with no backlog growth at all across the session.
- **The A2UI pipeline works end to end** — validated, no problems, surface emitted. What looked like a
  failure was the model emitting a *minimal* surface (a heading and two text lines) and putting the real
  content in speech. That is a prompt-quality problem, and the straddle risk everyone expected was never
  exercised because no large surface was ever produced.
- **About 8 % of the human's audio was replaced by silence**: 2081 capture xruns on turn 1 alone, ~21 s
  in a 260 s stretch. That belongs to the audio path and may be degrading recognition on its own.

## The cold-engine test (human scheduled it 2026-09-16, after T-0051)

The engine survived a full reply with the recogniser resident and **1.9 GiB free**, against a stated
3 GiB floor — but only as a **warm** engine, up nine hours, recycling its own arena. The case nobody
has run is a **cold boot with the recogniser already on the GPU**, which is what the next restart is,
and it is the moment the arena must be grown from the driver instead of recycled. Two minutes:
restart the engine with the recogniser left up, send one request, watch it complete or fail.

Until then **the KV pool stays at 4 GiB** (T-0048 ruling A13, reversing the shrink in ruling A2) and
T-0048's bench is rescoped to **measure the sampler's peak transient** rather than confirm a smaller
pool boots. One canvas-sized logits tensor is 0.25 GiB; the 2.5 GiB floor assumes ten live at once and
nothing has ever measured it.

## Queued follow-ups (small, each with its evidence already gathered)

Written 2026-09-16. None blocks anything; each was found by an agent that declined to fix it out of
scope, and each carries the reproduction that found it.

1. **One line, and the marker comes off in the same commit** (T-0043 rulings B1/B9/B17/B22).
   `cli.build_session` passes the log but not the log *directory*, so a session started with logging off
   has nowhere to write and the checkbox cannot be ticked. `tests/unit/test_cli_session.py::test_a_session_started_with_no_log_can_still_be_switched_on`
   is a **strict expected-failure** naming T-0049 group B as the owner, so it will fail loudly the moment
   the line lands — that is the point, and it is why the marker and the line are one commit. It passes in
   an isolated copy with the line added. Blocked only until T-0049 group B merges and frees `cli.py`.
2. **Seven rows inherit the human's shell** (T-0045 ruling A24): with `ASTRA_AUDIO_SOURCE` exported,
   three rows in `tests/contract/test_audio_device_placement.py` and four in
   `tests/unit/test_audio_device_quantum.py` go red, because the device layer reads the variable for
   itself. Same defect as the one T-0045 fixed, one layer out. Folded with item 3.
3. **Thirteen rows query the real audio graph** (T-0045 rulings A13/A16): three remain unpinned in
   modules other tasks own. The answer is box-dependent, so those rows run in a regime nobody states.
4. **Real-start rows in `tests/unit` reach the container daemon and the engine** (T-0045 ruling A22): a
   live `served_model=` was seen in a failure dump. Worse than item 3, since it talks to the engine.
5. **Two behaviours in T-0049 group B rest on judgement with no row** (rulings B11/B12): a failed start
   continuing to the next service, and a restart naming an unknown service exiting with a usage error
   having done nothing.
6. **A thread outlives its test, and it is a wrong-pass risk rather than a flake** (T-0049 ruling B14,
   sharpened by the group B review). The `astra-audio-writer` is a daemon thread that exits only after
   2 s of idle, polling every 2 ms — so a leaked one makes **~1000 clock reads into whatever the next
   module has patched**, for two seconds, in whichever module the shuffle puts next. It warns today only
   because no row patches the clock globally; the moment one does, or the moment a fake clock counts or
   advances on read, it becomes a wrong **pass**, not a red row. The cheap fix is an autouse teardown
   asserting no such thread outlives a test. **Do this before the fast suite is next used as the gate for
   a GPU run** — which is exactly what T-0048's bench will do.
7. **A cancellation race with a code-reviewed fix and no test** (T-0043 ruling B27). Closing a session
   mid-toggle used to lose the closing record and leave the log file open, unrepairable. The fix is in and
   reviewed; **no test exercises the race**, because the writer drains within one event-loop turn unless a
   harness holds it open, which is real work. Merged on that basis and recorded here rather than absorbed
   into a green suite. The harness needs a controllable suspension point inside the writer.
8. **T-0050, the serial-suite ceiling — now with a lead (T-0051 ruling A29).** Reproduced at **214 s
   twice on a quiet box**. The useful number is not the total: **user + system is ~125 s against ~214 s of
   wall**, so roughly **90 seconds is spent waiting rather than computing** — sleeps and timeouts, not
   contention. The same run emitted an unhandled-thread warning from the audio writer polling a clock,
   which is **the leaked thread queued as item 7**, so the two are plausibly related and the thread fix may
   be the cheaper first move. Original scope — and the drift is structural, not contention (T-0049 ruling B26).**
   Every serial number today was dismissed as contaminated, mine included. A verifier then measured
   **214.5 s twice** with the box's load average at 2.2–2.3, the least contended readings anyone took, and
   the task it was verifying accounts for ~17 s of that. So the gap against the 120 s ceiling is real, and
   the human's decision to make the parallel run canonical is better founded than when they made it. The
   change needs `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/tests.md`, `docs/09` section 3, the conftest constant and the
   budget test moved **together**: my first attempt changed two of five and the fence correctly went red.

   **Closed as a question, 2026-09-16, by T-0052's verifier — it is structural and it is not contention.**
   Two runs of the same selection at load averages differing **threefold** (7–10 and 2.2–3.6) gave
   **217.35 s and 214.11 s**, agreeing within **1.5 %**, at **57 % and 55 % CPU**. A run that is half idle at
   both extremes is waiting, not queuing behind other work. It also isolated T-0052's own new and changed
   modules at **0.68 s**, so the task under verification contributed essentially nothing. This corroborates
   ruling A29's split from the other direction and removes the excuse every report tonight reached for,
   mine included: the number was dismissed as contaminated all evening and it was never contaminated.
   The leaked audio-writer thread (item 7) remains the cheapest suspect, and it should be fixed **before**
   anyone tunes the ceiling, because a ceiling raised to accommodate a leak is a ceiling that hides it.

   **Third independent run, orchestrator, load average 0.56 — and it names a mechanism.** `/usr/bin/time -v`
   on the merged tree: **wall 212.37 s, user 108.53 s, system 9.83 s, 55 % CPU**, i.e. **94 seconds of the
   run is neither user nor system time**. That reproduces ruling A29's ~90 s from a third direction, on the
   quietest box anyone has measured. The new datum is **1 556 842 voluntary context switches** — about
   **7 300 per second of wall time**. Involuntary switches are 11 347, three orders of magnitude fewer, which
   is what rules contention out arithmetically rather than by argument: the process is not being preempted,
   it is **choosing to block**, over and over. That is the signature of polling and sleeping, not of a busy
   machine, and it is the strongest pointer yet at the leaked audio-writer thread and at whatever else
   sleeps in the subprocess-launching rows. Anyone hunting this should start by counting who blocks, not by
   profiling CPU: there is barely any CPU to profile.

   **And the suspect surfaced on its own, the same evening.** T-0051's implementer noticed a warning line
   appear and disappear between two runs of *identical* code and, instead of shrugging, re-ran with
   `-W always` and named it: `PytestUnhandledThreadExceptionWarning: Exception in thread
   **astra-audio-writer**` — a teardown race in the audio-writer thread, unrelated to whatever task happens
   to be running. That is **item 7's leaked thread**, now with a second, independent sighting and a
   reproduction switch. Taken with the context-switch arithmetic above, the case is: a thread that outlives
   its test, blocks on a clock, and is still there at teardown. It is flaky *and* it is the cheapest
   candidate for the 94 seconds. Fix the thread first; re-measure before touching the ceiling.

   **CORRECTION, same evening, by T-0057's planner — I told it not to assume the two facts were the same
   fact, and they are not.** Both halves are now measured. The thread is real and worse than thought:
   **48 of 66 rows** in four audio modules leak a writer (65 threads; one Hypothesis row leaves 29), each
   outliving its test by **2.00 s** at ~4 000 device reads a second, and those four modules alone produce
   **986 449 of the suite's 1 566 825 voluntary context switches — 63 % of all the blocking, from 66 of
   2 315 rows.** But removing the thread's lifetime moves the wall by **3.0 s of the 94, and plausibly
   none**: only 54 rows in the whole suite leak one, and 48 are already in those four modules. **~91 seconds
   is somewhere else.**

   **And my own arithmetic was misleading, which is the part to keep.** `/usr/bin/time` counts **child**
   CPU inside "User time" — verified directly, a parent idle at 0.00 while its child burns 0.84 s is
   reported as `user 0.84`. Measuring the pytest process's *own* CPU with `time.process_time()` gives
   **26.3 s across 211.4 s**. So "94 s is neither user nor system" was arithmetically true and pointed the
   wrong way: the honest statement is **pytest computes for ~27 s of a 212 s run and is idle ~88 % of it**,
   with roughly 91 s of the reported user time belonging to **subprocesses**. The waiting ranks by module —
   `test_audio_clock.py` 14.6 s, `test_stop_hook.py` 12.1 s, `test_audio_capture_clock.py` 10.4 s,
   `test_stt_client.py` 9.7 s — and only the first has real in-process CPU, which is the leaked threads
   burning it. **So the ceiling is not one bug.** T-0057 fixes a real defect and gates the switch count
   rather than the wall, precisely so that a suite still at ~212 s afterwards reads as the expected result
   and not as a failed fix.
8. **T-0043 ruling B10.** The log's opening record is specified to carry the session identity, commit,
   profile and lock digest; the writer emits the process id and the path.
9. **Start failures now print to stdout** (T-0049 group B, found in review, no criterion covers it): the
   old verb sent them to stderr, so a script watching stderr for failures would now see nothing. Nothing
   asserts either behaviour. Decide and pin it, or leave it deliberately.
10. **A cold start is now slower and that is by design** (T-0049 ruling B4): because each start waits for
   health before the next begins, a cold `astra up` starts the recogniser only after the engine answers,
   about 68 s in, where the old verb spawned all three at once and probed concurrently. That is what the
   architecture asks for and what B4 ratified, but the human will see a longer cold wall time and should
   not read it as a regression. No unit row exercises it; they all inject a probe.

11. **The interface repair budget silently truncates** (T-0052 ruling A7, measured 2026-09-16 by running the
   real `UiGate` over the flight fixture): six leaf components of the human's flight surface fail the schema,
   and `max_resamples=3`, so the gate requested `src`, `dest` and `date1` and **silently did not request**
   `date2`, `passengers` or `search`. They are not rendered, not requested and not reported. A surface that
   needs more than three repairs can therefore never become complete however many canvases arrive. It is
   `ui_gate.py` (T-0010's file), which T-0052 is forbidden to touch. The same probe shows why the leaves fail:
   the model wrote `{"id":"date1","component":"DateTimeInput"}` with **no required properties at all**, so the
   catalog block in the prompt is not telling it what a leaf component needs — which points at T-0044's file
   as much as at the gate.

13. **Sentence splitting ends on ASCII punctuation only, so in CJK a whole answer is one sentence**
   (T-0052 ruling A22, measured by a reviewer 2026-09-16). `speech_gate._split` terminates on `.`, `!`
   and `?`; `。` is not a terminator. A 59-character Japanese answer therefore arrives as **one**
   source, and anything that acts per sentence acts on the whole turn instead. It surfaced because the
   voice guard silenced a Japanese answer whole where it would have dropped one English sentence, but
   the defect is independent of the guard: **every per-sentence cost model in the project is a per-turn
   cost in CJK**, including the speech gate's own "a refusal loses one sentence" reasoning and anything
   downstream that counts sentences. `docs/01-design.md` does not scope the product to English. Needs
   its own task: the terminator set, the regime it was measured in, and a row per script.

12. **Three fields of the turn record are declared, pinned by a test, and never written** (found by the
   T-0053 planner in the memory-leak log, verified across two sessions 2026-09-16). `thought_tokens`,
   `answer_tokens` and `thought_closed` are declared in `local_astra/telemetry/turns_log.py:74-76` with
   defaults of `0`, `0` and `None`, and **nothing in `local_astra/` ever assigns them** — grep finds the
   declaration and no writer. Measured: in `logs/sessions/20260916-211716-1250570.jsonl`, all **five** turns
   record `thought_tokens: 0, answer_tokens: 0, thought_closed: null` despite 14 `llm.commit` records and
   2 to 3 canvases each; the earlier `20260916-202852-987340.jsonl` turn does the same with 4 canvases.
   Worse, `tests/unit/test_session_record.py:313` pins the zeros as expected output, so the suite currently
   **certifies** the empty counters — the "no asserting current behaviour captured from the implementation"
   rule in `.claude/rules/tests.md`, applied to the exact case it exists for. These are the counters any
   later answer-length or thought-length threshold would read out of `turns.jsonl`, so they should be filled
   before a bench depends on them. (`prefix_tokens: 0` is **not** part of this: `session.py:1327` documents
   it as "the engine did not tell us", which is a true statement about a missing `stream_options`.)

14. **A turn-machine invariant failed once under Hypothesis (found 2026-09-30 by the T-0066 verifier; not yet triaged).**
   `tests/unit/test_turn_invariants.py::TestTurnMachineInvariants::runTest`, serial `-p no:randomly` run on the
   T-0066 tree (no diff under `local_astra/` vs main): `gate.pending 'The error is on' is not a prefix of the machine's unspoken
   tail 'Yes. The error is on'`, shrunk to `whole_turn_up_to_thinking_or_speaking(answers=False, text='Yes. ')`. Failed again on the immediate
   rerun (saved example), passed 3/3 after the `.hypothesis` database was cleared and 9/9 on main — so it is a rare
   path, not a T-0062-tree regression. docs/02 §4 (gate vs held text). Next step: `/triage` — pin the shrunk case
   as an explicit `@example` so it fails deterministically, then fix.
